I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Like if I think of a song he liked, or someone I ought to call, I want to do it right then so it doesn’t get missed.\n\nI suppose it has gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m doing something for it—calling folks, chasing down music, planning displays, even thinking about trips. My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group and almost everything else slide. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, but it feels like a good thing I’m trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. Maybe a little uneasy, like I’m forgetting something I meant to do for him. But it’s not as though I’m sitting there punishing myself over it.\n\nI just… I suppose I feel better when I’m making progress on it. That sounds all right, doesn’t it? Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I want it to be nice, and respectful… I’d hate for it to look careless. But I don’t think I’m expecting some impossible standard.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Anxious… maybe a little, yes. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s more that when someone steps in, I feel this push inside me, like I need them to see what I’m trying to do before they start changing things.

Sometimes I feel almost… lit up by it, full of purpose, like this memorial could finally make the whole town understand our family properly. And then if my wife, or someone at the senior center, says, “Maybe don’t do it that way,” I can get sharp. I don’t mean to. I’ll snap a bit if they slow me down or question the display.

That’s not me trying to be ungrateful. I know people mean well. I just… I get carried along by it, I suppose.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say no one else could do it correctly… that sounds a bit harsh, doesn’t it?

It’s more that this has become… well, bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.

I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.

But I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.

So if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?

But it’s become a lot of chasing down particular songs, calling people, arranging little displays, even thinking about trips connected to it. Most days I’m doing something for it.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let it crowd out the garden and my walking group and… well, most other things. Maybe she’s right, though I’d like to think I’m just trying to make it meaningful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s more that I want to, mostly. I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Like if I think of a song he liked, or someone I ought to call, I want to do it right then so it doesn’t get missed.\n\nI suppose it has gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m doing something for it—calling folks, chasing down music, planning displays, even thinking about trips. My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group and almost everything else slide. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, but it feels like a good thing I’m trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. Maybe a little uneasy, like I’m forgetting something I meant to do for him. But it’s not as though I’m sitting there punishing myself over it.\n\nI just… I suppose I feel better when I’m making progress on it. That sounds all right, doesn’t it? Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. It jumps around like that, and I can’t always slow it down enough to write it all in proper order.\n\nThat’s not too strange, is it? I mean, given what it’s for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everything about the memorial has to be perfect?\n\nPatient: Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I want it to be nice, and respectful… I’d hate for it to look careless. But I don’t think I’m expecting some impossible standard.\n\nMaybe I do fuss over parts of it a bit—what song goes where, whether I’ve called the right people—but that’s just wanting it done properly, isn’t it? I suppose I want it to feel right more than perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be in charge of every aspect of the memorial?\n\nPatient: In charge of every aspect… well, I wouldn’t say I need to be, exactly. I just seem to end up holding most of the pieces, if that makes sense.\n\nIt started as a simple memorial for my brother, but it’s gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m chasing down songs, calling people, thinking about displays, even planning little trips to find things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and, well, nearly everything else.\n\nI don’t mean to be difficult about it. I just feel like if I don’t keep it moving, it won’t come together right. That sounds understandable, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when someone else tries to help you with the memorial?\n\nPatient: Anxious… maybe a little, yes. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s more that when someone steps in, I feel this push inside me, like I need them to see what I’m trying to do before they start changing things.\n\nSometimes I feel almost… lit up by it, full of purpose, like this memorial could finally make the whole town understand our family properly. And then if my wife, or someone at the senior center, says, “Maybe don’t do it that way,” I can get sharp. I don’t mean to. I’ll snap a bit if they slow me down or question the display.\n\nThat’s not me trying to be ungrateful. I know people mean well. I just… I get carried along by it, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial would not be done correctly if someone else were in charge of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say no one else could do it correctly… that sounds a bit harsh, doesn’t it?\n\nIt’s more that this has become… well, bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else slide, and maybe she’s right. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.

But honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. So if someone took it over, I think I’d feel more uncomfortable than relieved.

My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else, and… well, she’s not wrong. I know that doesn’t sound ideal. I just feel like I’m in the middle of it, and stepping away would be hard.

It started out as something simple, but it’s grown quite a bit bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days calling people, tracking down songs, planning little displays and even some trips connected to it.\n\nMy wife thinks I’ve let it take over a bit—she says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and, well, most other things. I don’t mean for it to be a problem, but she thought it might be good for me to come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting very caught up in the small details of the memorial?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do get a bit caught up in them. I don’t mean it in a bad way, you know—it feels important to get things right for my brother. But it’s become a lot of chasing down particular songs, calling people, arranging little displays, even thinking about trips connected to it. Most days I’m doing something for it.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let it crowd out the garden and my walking group and… well, most other things. Maybe she’s right, though I’d like to think I’m just trying to make it meaningful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s more that I want to, mostly. I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Like if I think of a song he liked, or someone I ought to call, I want to do it right then so it doesn’t get missed.\n\nI suppose it has gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m doing something for it—calling folks, chasing down music, planning displays, even thinking about trips. My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group and almost everything else slide. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, but it feels like a good thing I’m trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. Maybe a little uneasy, like I’m forgetting something I meant to do for him. But it’s not as though I’m sitting there punishing myself over it.\n\nI just… I suppose I feel better when I’m making progress on it. That sounds all right, doesn’t it? Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. It jumps around like that, and I can’t always slow it down enough to write it all in proper order.\n\nThat’s not too strange, is it? I mean, given what it’s for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everything about the memorial has to be perfect?\n\nPatient: Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I want it to be nice, and respectful… I’d hate for it to look careless. But I don’t think I’m expecting some impossible standard.\n\nMaybe I do fuss over parts of it a bit—what song goes where, whether I’ve called the right people—but that’s just wanting it done properly, isn’t it? I suppose I want it to feel right more than perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be in charge of every aspect of the memorial?\n\nPatient: In charge of every aspect… well, I wouldn’t say I need to be, exactly. I just seem to end up holding most of the pieces, if that makes sense.\n\nIt started as a simple memorial for my brother, but it’s gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m chasing down songs, calling people, thinking about displays, even planning little trips to find things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and, well, nearly everything else.\n\nI don’t mean to be difficult about it. I just feel like if I don’t keep it moving, it won’t come together right. That sounds understandable, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when someone else tries to help you with the memorial?\n\nPatient: Anxious… maybe a little, yes. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s more that when someone steps in, I feel this push inside me, like I need them to see what I’m trying to do before they start changing things.\n\nSometimes I feel almost… lit up by it, full of purpose, like this memorial could finally make the whole town understand our family properly. And then if my wife, or someone at the senior center, says, “Maybe don’t do it that way,” I can get sharp. I don’t mean to. I’ll snap a bit if they slow me down or question the display.\n\nThat’s not me trying to be ungrateful. I know people mean well. I just… I get carried along by it, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial would not be done correctly if someone else were in charge of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say no one else could do it correctly… that sounds a bit harsh, doesn’t it?\n\nIt’s more that this has become… well, bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else slide, and maybe she’s right. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. So if someone took it over, I think I’d feel more uncomfortable than relieved.\n\nMy wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else, and… well, she’s not wrong. I know that doesn’t sound ideal. I just feel like I’m in the middle of it, and stepping away would be hard.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

Yes… I suppose I do, though I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic.

It’s not always that I’m sitting there choosing to think about it. My mind just keeps jumping around—one minute I’m picturing a display case, then I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from an old ballad comes into my head. And before I can write one thing down properly, I’m already onto the next.

I know everyone gets busy thoughts sometimes, but this feels a bit hard to slow down. Is that the sort of thing you mean?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I think that does happen.

I’ll try to be thinking about something ordinary, and then my mind jumps right back to the memorial. Not even in a neat way, really. It goes from one idea to the next—like a display case, then maybe taking a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad—and I can’t quite slow it down enough to write it all in order.

I know that probably sounds a bit scattered. I’m trying to explain it properly.

My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group and almost everything else slide. I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. Maybe a little uneasy, like I’m forgetting something I meant to do for him. But it’s not as though I’m sitting there punishing myself over it.\n\nI just… I suppose I feel better when I’m making progress on it. That sounds all right, doesn’t it? Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. I mean, given what it’s for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everything about the memorial has to be perfect?\n\nPatient: Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. I don’t mean for it to be a problem, but she thought it might be good for me to come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting very caught up in the small details of the memorial?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do get a bit caught up in them. I don’t mean it in a bad way, you know—it feels important to get things right for my brother. But it’s become a lot of chasing down particular songs, calling people, arranging little displays, even thinking about trips connected to it. Most days I’m doing something for it.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let it crowd out the garden and my walking group and… well, most other things. Maybe she’s right, though I’d like to think I’m just trying to make it meaningful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s more that I want to, mostly. I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? I suppose I want it to feel right more than perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be in charge of every aspect of the memorial?\n\nPatient: In charge of every aspect… well, I wouldn’t say I need to be, exactly. I just seem to end up holding most of the pieces, if that makes sense.\n\nIt started as a simple memorial for my brother, but it’s gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m chasing down songs, calling people, thinking about displays, even planning little trips to find things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and, well, nearly everything else.\n\nI don’t mean to be difficult about it. I just feel like if I don’t keep it moving, it won’t come together right. Yes… I think they do interrupt, though I don’t mean it like I’m completely unable to think.

I don’t mean for it to be a problem, but she thought it might be good for me to come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting very caught up in the small details of the memorial?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do get a bit caught up in them. I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Like if I think of a song he liked, or someone I ought to call, I want to do it right then so it doesn’t get missed.\n\nI suppose it has gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m doing something for it—calling folks, chasing down music, planning displays, even thinking about trips. My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group and almost everything else slide. I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. Maybe a little uneasy, like I’m forgetting something I meant to do for him. But it’s not as though I’m sitting there punishing myself over it.\n\nI just… I suppose I feel better when I’m making progress on it. That sounds all right, doesn’t it? Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. It jumps around like that, and I can’t always slow it down enough to write it all in proper order.\n\nThat’s not too strange, is it? I mean, given what it’s for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everything about the memorial has to be perfect?\n\nPatient: Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I want it to be nice, and respectful… I’d hate for it to look careless. But I don’t think I’m expecting some impossible standard.\n\nMaybe I do fuss over parts of it a bit—what song goes where, whether I’ve called the right people—but that’s just wanting it done properly, isn’t it? I suppose I want it to feel right more than perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be in charge of every aspect of the memorial?\n\nPatient: In charge of every aspect… well, I wouldn’t say I need to be, exactly. I just seem to end up holding most of the pieces, if that makes sense.\n\nIt started as a simple memorial for my brother, but it’s gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m chasing down songs, calling people, thinking about displays, even planning little trips to find things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and, well, nearly everything else.\n\nI don’t mean to be difficult about it. I just feel like if I don’t keep it moving, it won’t come together right. That sounds understandable, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when someone else tries to help you with the memorial?\n\nPatient: Anxious… maybe a little, yes. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s more that when someone steps in, I feel this push inside me, like I need them to see what I’m trying to do before they start changing things.\n\nSometimes I feel almost… lit up by it, full of purpose, like this memorial could finally make the whole town understand our family properly. And then if my wife, or someone at the senior center, says, “Maybe don’t do it that way,” I can get sharp. I don’t mean to. I’ll snap a bit if they slow me down or question the display.\n\nThat’s not me trying to be ungrateful. I know people mean well. I just… I get carried along by it, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial would not be done correctly if someone else were in charge of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say no one else could do it correctly… that sounds a bit harsh, doesn’t it?\n\nIt’s more that this has become… well, bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else slide, and maybe she’s right. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. So if someone took it over, I think I’d feel more uncomfortable than relieved.\n\nMy wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else, and… well, she’s not wrong. I know that doesn’t sound ideal. I just feel like I’m in the middle of it, and stepping away would be hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the memorial even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, though I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m sitting there choosing to think about it. My mind just keeps jumping around—one minute I’m picturing a display case, then I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from an old ballad comes into my head. And before I can write one thing down properly, I’m already onto the next.\n\nI know everyone gets busy thoughts sometimes, but this feels a bit hard to slow down. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. My mind just keeps jumping around—one minute I’m picturing a display case, then I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from an old ballad comes into my head. And before I can write one thing down properly, I’m already onto the next.\n\nI know everyone gets busy thoughts sometimes, but this feels a bit hard to slow down. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial come into your mind even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that does happen.\n\nI’ll try to be thinking about something ordinary, and then my mind jumps right back to the memorial. Not even in a neat way, really. It goes from one idea to the next—like a display case, then maybe taking a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad—and I can’t quite slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nI know that probably sounds a bit scattered. I’m trying to explain it properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are coming into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “against my will” exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I don’t mean for it to be a problem, but she thought it might be good for me to come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting very caught up in the small details of the memorial?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do get a bit caught up in them. I don’t mean it in a bad way, you know—it feels important to get things right for my brother. But it’s become a lot of chasing down particular songs, calling people, arranging little displays, even thinking about trips connected to it. Most days I’m doing something for it.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let it crowd out the garden and my walking group and… well, most other things. Maybe she’s right, though I’d like to think I’m just trying to make it meaningful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s more that I want to, mostly. I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Like if I think of a song he liked, or someone I ought to call, I want to do it right then so it doesn’t get missed.\n\nI suppose it has gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m doing something for it—calling folks, chasing down music, planning displays, even thinking about trips. My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group and almost everything else slide. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, but it feels like a good thing I’m trying to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. Maybe a little uneasy, like I’m forgetting something I meant to do for him. But it’s not as though I’m sitting there punishing myself over it.\n\nI just… I suppose I feel better when I’m making progress on it. That sounds all right, doesn’t it? Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. It jumps around like that, and I can’t always slow it down enough to write it all in proper order.\n\nThat’s not too strange, is it? I mean, given what it’s for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everything about the memorial has to be perfect?\n\nPatient: Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I want it to be nice, and respectful… I’d hate for it to look careless. But I don’t think I’m expecting some impossible standard.\n\nMaybe I do fuss over parts of it a bit—what song goes where, whether I’ve called the right people—but that’s just wanting it done properly, isn’t it? I suppose I want it to feel right more than perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be in charge of every aspect of the memorial?\n\nPatient: In charge of every aspect… well, I wouldn’t say I need to be, exactly. I just seem to end up holding most of the pieces, if that makes sense.\n\nIt started as a simple memorial for my brother, but it’s gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m chasing down songs, calling people, thinking about displays, even planning little trips to find things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and, well, nearly everything else.\n\nI don’t mean to be difficult about it. I just feel like if I don’t keep it moving, it won’t come together right. That sounds understandable, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when someone else tries to help you with the memorial?\n\nPatient: Anxious… maybe a little, yes. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s more that when someone steps in, I feel this push inside me, like I need them to see what I’m trying to do before they start changing things.\n\nSometimes I feel almost… lit up by it, full of purpose, like this memorial could finally make the whole town understand our family properly. And then if my wife, or someone at the senior center, says, “Maybe don’t do it that way,” I can get sharp. I don’t mean to. I’ll snap a bit if they slow me down or question the display.\n\nThat’s not me trying to be ungrateful. I know people mean well. I just… I get carried along by it, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial would not be done correctly if someone else were in charge of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say no one else could do it correctly… that sounds a bit harsh, doesn’t it?\n\nIt’s more that this has become… well, bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else slide, and maybe she’s right. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. So if someone took it over, I think I’d feel more uncomfortable than relieved.\n\nMy wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else, and… well, she’s not wrong. I know that doesn’t sound ideal. I just feel like I’m in the middle of it, and stepping away would be hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the memorial even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, though I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m sitting there choosing to think about it. My mind just keeps jumping around—one minute I’m picturing a display case, then I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from an old ballad comes into my head. And before I can write one thing down properly, I’m already onto the next.\n\nI know everyone gets busy thoughts sometimes, but this feels a bit hard to slow down. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial come into your mind even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that does happen.\n\nI’ll try to be thinking about something ordinary, and then my mind jumps right back to the memorial. Not even in a neat way, really. It goes from one idea to the next—like a display case, then maybe taking a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad—and I can’t quite slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nI know that probably sounds a bit scattered. I’m trying to explain it properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are coming into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “against my will” exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yes, they do seem to come in when I’m not meaning to think about them.\n\nIt’s like my mind keeps jumping from one memorial idea to the next—one minute the display case, then I’m thinking about a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad comes in—and I can’t slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nDoes that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are interrupting your other thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do interrupt, though I don’t mean it like I’m completely unable to think.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll be on one thing, and then suddenly I’m back on the memorial again—my mind jumps to the display case, then to whether I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from a ballad comes in. And I can’t slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m explaining that right, but yes, it does cut across my other thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thinking about the memorial is causing you a lot of emotional pain?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it a lot of emotional pain… I mean, it does bother me some, yes. It can weigh on me when I keep going back over it.\n\nBut I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like it gets under my skin and makes me feel uneasy, or sad maybe, rather than… well, I don’t know what level you mean by “a lot.” Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. I guess… yes, some of them are unwanted, in the sense that I’m not choosing to bring them up right then.

It’s not that I hate every thought about it or anything. But my mind keeps jumping from one thing to the next—one minute it’s the display case, then maybe taking a road trip to find a cousin, then a verse from a ballad—and I can’t slow it down enough to write it in order.

So, yes… they come in when I’d rather they didn’t. Is that what you mean?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it’s mostly about this memorial I’m putting together for my brother. It started out as something simple, but it’s grown quite a bit bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days calling people, tracking down songs, planning little displays and even some trips connected to it.\n\nMy wife thinks I’ve let it take over a bit—she says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group, and, well, most other things. I don’t mean for it to be a problem, but she thought it might be good for me to come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting very caught up in the small details of the memorial?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I do get a bit caught up in them. I don’t mean it in a bad way, you know—it feels important to get things right for my brother. But it’s become a lot of chasing down particular songs, calling people, arranging little displays, even thinking about trips connected to it. Maybe she’s right, though I’d like to think I’m just trying to make it meaningful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or is it more that you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s more that I want to, mostly. I don’t feel like someone’s forcing me, exactly. It just feels… important, you know? Like if I think of a song he liked, or someone I ought to call, I want to do it right then so it doesn’t get missed.\n\nI suppose it has gotten bigger than I meant it to. Like a normal thing, wanting to do right by someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about the memorial even when you're not actively working on it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, though I try not to let it sound like I’m obsessed with it. It’s more like my mind keeps picking it up again, even when I’m meant to be doing something else.\n\nOne minute I’ll be thinking about a display case, then suddenly I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then a line from some old ballad comes into my head. It jumps around like that, and I can’t always slow it down enough to write it all in proper order.\n\nThat’s not too strange, is it? I mean, given what it’s for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like everything about the memorial has to be perfect?\n\nPatient: Perfect? I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly. I mean, I want it to be nice, and respectful… I’d hate for it to look careless. But I don’t think I’m expecting some impossible standard.\n\nMaybe I do fuss over parts of it a bit—what song goes where, whether I’ve called the right people—but that’s just wanting it done properly, isn’t it? I suppose I want it to feel right more than perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be in charge of every aspect of the memorial?\n\nPatient: In charge of every aspect… well, I wouldn’t say I need to be, exactly. I just seem to end up holding most of the pieces, if that makes sense.\n\nIt started as a simple memorial for my brother, but it’s gotten bigger than I meant it to. Most days I’m chasing down songs, calling people, thinking about displays, even planning little trips to find things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and, well, nearly everything else.\n\nI don’t mean to be difficult about it. I just feel like if I don’t keep it moving, it won’t come together right. That sounds understandable, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when someone else tries to help you with the memorial?\n\nPatient: Anxious… maybe a little, yes. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s more that when someone steps in, I feel this push inside me, like I need them to see what I’m trying to do before they start changing things.\n\nSometimes I feel almost… lit up by it, full of purpose, like this memorial could finally make the whole town understand our family properly. And then if my wife, or someone at the senior center, says, “Maybe don’t do it that way,” I can get sharp. I don’t mean to. I’ll snap a bit if they slow me down or question the display.\n\nThat’s not me trying to be ungrateful. I know people mean well. I just… I get carried along by it, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial would not be done correctly if someone else were in charge of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say no one else could do it correctly… that sounds a bit harsh, doesn’t it?\n\nIt’s more that this has become… well, bigger than I meant it to. I’ve been spending most days on it now—chasing down the right songs, calling people, thinking through displays, even planning little trips to find things or speak to folks. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else slide, and maybe she’s right. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. So if someone took it over, I think I’d feel more uncomfortable than relieved.\n\nMy wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else, and… well, she’s not wrong. I know that doesn’t sound ideal. I just feel like I’m in the middle of it, and stepping away would be hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the memorial even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, though I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m sitting there choosing to think about it. My mind just keeps jumping around—one minute I’m picturing a display case, then I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from an old ballad comes into my head. And before I can write one thing down properly, I’m already onto the next.\n\nI know everyone gets busy thoughts sometimes, but this feels a bit hard to slow down. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial come into your mind even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that does happen.\n\nI’ll try to be thinking about something ordinary, and then my mind jumps right back to the memorial. Not even in a neat way, really. It goes from one idea to the next—like a display case, then maybe taking a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad—and I can’t quite slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nI know that probably sounds a bit scattered. I’m trying to explain it properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are coming into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “against my will” exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yes, they do seem to come in when I’m not meaning to think about them.\n\nIt’s like my mind keeps jumping from one memorial idea to the next—one minute the display case, then I’m thinking about a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad comes in—and I can’t slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nDoes that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are interrupting your other thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do interrupt, though I don’t mean it like I’m completely unable to think.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll be on one thing, and then suddenly I’m back on the memorial again—my mind jumps to the display case, then to whether I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from a ballad comes in. It can weigh on me when I keep going back over it.\n\nBut I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like it gets under my skin and makes me feel uneasy, or sad maybe, rather than… well, I don’t know what level you mean by “a lot.” Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are popping into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose they do pop in… I’m not trying to sit there and dwell on it, exactly.\n\nIt’s more like my mind just keeps jumping—first I’m picturing the display case, then I’m thinking maybe I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad comes up. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are forcing their way into your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think “forcing” might be a little strong, maybe, but they do come in pretty hard sometimes.\n\nIt’s like my mind keeps jumping from one idea to the next—the display case, then I’m suddenly thinking about a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from a ballad—and I can’t slow it down enough to get it written in order.\n\nSo… yes, in that way, they kind of push their way in. And I suppose because I’ve got all those pieces in my head, it’s hard to imagine handing it over and feeling sure it would come out the way it should.\n\nMy wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else slide, and maybe she’s right. I just feel responsible for it, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be unable to cope if someone else took over planning the memorial?\n\nPatient: Unable to cope… I don’t think I’d fall apart, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut I suppose I’d have a hard time with it. This memorial for my brother has just gotten bigger than I meant, and I’ve been spending most days on it now—finding songs, calling people, planning displays, thinking about little trips to get things or talk to folks. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and just about everything else.\n\nSo if someone else took over completely, I think I’d feel… unsettled. Like I wouldn’t know where to put myself, maybe. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be relieved if someone else took over the planning of the memorial and you didn't have to be in charge of it?\n\nPatient: Relieved… I suppose part of me should feel that way, shouldn’t I? It would probably be sensible to let someone else carry some of it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t think relief is the first thing I’d feel. This memorial for my brother has sort of grown bigger than I meant it to, and I’ve been putting most days into it now—chasing down songs, calling folks, planning displays, even thinking about trips to get things right. So if someone took it over, I think I’d feel more uncomfortable than relieved.\n\nMy wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and nearly everything else, and… well, she’s not wrong. I know that doesn’t sound ideal. I just feel like I’m in the middle of it, and stepping away would be hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the memorial even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, though I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic.\n\nIt’s not always that I’m sitting there choosing to think about it. My mind just keeps jumping around—one minute I’m picturing a display case, then I’m wondering if I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from an old ballad comes into my head. And before I can write one thing down properly, I’m already onto the next.\n\nI know everyone gets busy thoughts sometimes, but this feels a bit hard to slow down. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial come into your mind even when you're trying to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that does happen.\n\nI’ll try to be thinking about something ordinary, and then my mind jumps right back to the memorial. Not even in a neat way, really. It goes from one idea to the next—like a display case, then maybe taking a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad—and I can’t quite slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nI know that probably sounds a bit scattered. I’m trying to explain it properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are coming into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “against my will” exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yes, they do seem to come in when I’m not meaning to think about them.\n\nIt’s like my mind keeps jumping from one memorial idea to the next—one minute the display case, then I’m thinking about a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad comes in—and I can’t slow it down enough to write it all in order.\n\nDoes that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are interrupting your other thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do interrupt, though I don’t mean it like I’m completely unable to think.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll be on one thing, and then suddenly I’m back on the memorial again—my mind jumps to the display case, then to whether I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some line from a ballad comes in. It can weigh on me when I keep going back over it.\n\nBut I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like it gets under my skin and makes me feel uneasy, or sad maybe, rather than… well, I don’t know what level you mean by “a lot.” Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about the memorial are popping into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose they do pop in… I’m not trying to sit there and dwell on it, exactly.\n\nIt’s more like my mind just keeps jumping—first I’m picturing the display case, then I’m thinking maybe I should take a road trip to find a cousin, then some verse from an old ballad comes up.
